# Mounted Endpoint Index — Pass 14 Coverage Report

## A. Executive summary

Chapter 14 currently contains **595 distinct method/full-path rows**. Its prose says 594 registrations and 332 first-batch frontend routes, but the table parses to **595 unique rows** and **333 frontend-facing + 79 staff-facing = 412 first-batch rows**. The source report therefore has a one-row internal count discrepancy; this report preserves the handoff and records the discrepancy instead of editing it.

| Chapter 14 table classification | Rows |
|---|---:|
| First-batch frontend-facing | 333 |
| First-batch staff-facing | 79 |
| Legacy/compatibility alias | 66 |
| Legacy/deprecated | 4 |
| Operational/internal | 51 |
| Provider callback | 3 |
| Unclear | 55 |
| Unclear (staff/admin scope) | 4 |
| **Parsed total** | **595** |

The source scan found **348 HTTP call-to-route mappings** from **40 source files**, representing **337 normalized method/path patterns** and **336 distinct Chapter 14 rows used**. All mapped patterns match a mounted method/path row; **0 unmatched API route expressions** were found. One conditional method alias expands one source invocation to both documented PUT and PATCH variants. The scan does not prove live availability or that every wrapper is reachable in a user journey.

Direct API call sites use **287/412** first-batch routes; **125** first-batch rows have no direct call in the scanned frontend. Those are candidates for intentional non-use, shared/out-of-scope behavior, incomplete journeys or frontend gaps; they are not automatically defects. Overall, **259** mounted rows have no direct frontend call, including internal, provider-callback, alias, deprecated and unclear-scope routes.

The reported Pass 13 `222 of 411` documentation-gap count does not reproduce from the current local files. With a conservative literal-path scan across the listed feature/supporting chapters (excluding Chapter 14), **291/412** first-batch paths occur somewhere and **277/412** have the exact method and literal route path on the same line; **135/412** lack that exact method/path pair by this test. Exact-path presence is only a documentation locator, not evidence that DTO, permission, error and state-machine contracts are complete. Chapter 14 explicitly says those details remain in the feature chapter/controller/service.

Two frontend-owned security corrections were made: title-verification review/retry/revoke/external-anchor service calls now require and bind a step-up challenge, and the protected title-document content client now accepts only the documented viewer-content route shape. Focused tests, no-emit TypeScript and edited-file lint pass. No live backend requests were made.

## B. Mounted endpoint inventory

The final route-match status reports source alignment only: a matched row has a direct frontend call; a no-call row remains classified by Chapter 14 scope and must not be treated automatically as a frontend defect or as complete.
IDs `EP-0001` through `EP-0595` follow Chapter 14 table order. Route parameters are copied from the table. `/api` is the API prefix for all listed paths. Chapter 14 does **not** itself specify per-route query parameters, content type, auth/profile requirements, step-up, versions, request DTOs or response DTOs; those are intentionally marked as requiring the linked feature guide/DTO chapters rather than inferred from a route name. `C14 line` gives the exact inventory row.

**Per-route request/security status:** Chapter 14 is an endpoint index, not the request/response contract. Query parameters, request media type, auth, role/permission, active-profile context, step-up, expected version/revision and DTO fields must be read from the route’s `Contract guide` plus Chapters 15–16. Where that guide does not specify an item, it remains a contract gap; no value is inferred here. For actual frontend request expressions, see section C. The test column is a static source scan for route literals in test files and does not claim the test fully asserts all DTO/security semantics.

## D. Bidirectional endpoint coverage

### Frontend to handoff

- **Matched:** 337 normalized method/path patterns map to **336** distinct mounted rows. All method/path expressions matched after normalizing concrete identifiers to route parameters and expanding finite static branches.
- **Unmatched:** 0 API route expressions. No frontend call was found without a corresponding Chapter 14 method/path row.
- **Potentially ambiguous:** 0 call mappings matched equal-scored route patterns; these are listed in source inventory by endpoint ID and should be checked if a path family contains aliases.
- **Request/response contract completeness:** matching a route does not establish the request DTO, response projection, auth policy or state machine; those are assessed separately below.

### Handoff to frontend

- **Documented and directly used:** 336 endpoint rows.
- **First-batch rows with no direct frontend call:** 125 of 412 (see Appendix B for every row and the linked guide). These are not automatically bugs: some may be reached through a shared backend operation, intentionally not exposed, blocked by an incomplete contract, or not yet connected to UI.
- **All documented rows with no direct frontend call:** 259 of 595. This includes source-informed aliases, deprecated rows, provider callbacks and operational routes that are not expected to be browser API calls, as well as unclear and potentially first-batch rows.
- **Available service but no UI:** not inferred solely from route/service files. The API usage appendix identifies wrappers; the route/UI consumer must be verified before a row is marked journey-complete.
- **Intentionally excluded:** provider callbacks, operational/internal routes, deprecated routes, compatibility aliases, and unsupported product routes are not exposed as ordinary frontend actions unless the corresponding feature contract calls for them. In particular, protected payment CSV export remains `POST /api/payments/export.csv`; the legacy GET route is not used for the protected export.

## E. Request contract findings

- API prefix: `src/lib/axios.ts` configures the shared client with the API base; service paths omit `/api`, and the request interceptor adds bearer authorization when a token is present plus `X-Profile-ID` when an active profile exists.
- The call inventory records first argument, body expression and third/config argument. `params` and headers are visible in those expressions; the endpoint index itself does not define query/body/header schemas.
- Literal route construction was normalized without equating different HTTP methods. Concrete identifiers were converted to path parameters for matching. The two `saveReport` branches were expanded to both documented `PUT` and `PATCH` routes.
- Multipart request bodies were not treated as JSON. The corrected title-document upload continues to bind the actual FormData metadata/file digest through the documented challenge protocol. Uploads unrelated to that contract were not altered.
- No unsupported request field, API path or endpoint was added. The title-verification challenge requirement was tightened to require a non-empty challenge and `X-Step-Up-Challenge` on the protected staff mutations documented in Chapter 06.
- Route-specific field/version/idempotency requirements are listed in the linked feature chapters; Chapter 14 does not repeat them. Missing specifics remain blockers rather than inferred request-body fields.

## F. Response contract findings

- The frontend has endpoint-specific typed response expectations in service wrappers, but response variations are not uniform. Chapter 16 is a selective variation matrix; a path’s absence there is not evidence that the endpoint returns no body.
- No response was defaulted to a fabricated zero/empty success value as part of this pass.
- Async 202/job outcomes and financial unknown outcomes continue to depend on authoritative status reads; a route match does not make acceptance equal completion.
- The protected title-document content path is now normalized through an allowlist to the documented viewer route, rather than sending the bearer-auth Axios client to an arbitrary absolute or same-origin response URL. The response-provided viewer session token remains the path parameter.

## G. Security and step-up findings

- The shared API interceptor applies the existing bearer and active-profile headers; HTTP 401 cleanup is handled separately from 403 permission errors. This is frontend source evidence, not a substitute for backend authorization.
- `reviewTitleVerification`, `retryTitleVerificationExternalAnchor`, and `revokeTitleVerification` now reject an empty challenge and send `X-Step-Up-Challenge`. The admin review component passes the actual challenge ID in the corrected argument position.
- `requestRegistryExternalAnchor` was also checked and requires the documented challenge. No challenge is reused across a changed payload.
- Step-up call shape/body binding remains owned by the existing bound step-up helper. This pass did not change the helper or invent a route.
- Private title-document content retrieval only accepts the documented viewer content path. Protocol-relative URLs, unrelated same-origin paths, query/hash-bearing paths and arbitrary external URLs are rejected before Axios sends credentials.
- Chapter 14 has no per-route permission table. Role and resource authorization must be validated against the feature chapter and backend at live acceptance; no permission names were invented.

## H. Financial safety findings

- Frontend call paths were checked against the mounted methods. Payment initialization, verification and reconciliation use distinct documented routes; their state machines are not collapsed.
- The protected CSV export contract is `POST /api/payments/export.csv`; the legacy `GET /api/payments/export.csv` route is intentionally not used by the protected frontend export.
- No live payment, refund, escrow release, cancellation or export request was initiated. No ambiguous payment mutation is automatically retried by this pass.
- Ownership remains sourced from backend ownership projections; a payment attempt or redirect is not treated as confirmed ownership.
- Payment-attempt uniqueness-index deployment and provider/webhook acceptance remain operational dependencies reported in Pass 12/13; they cannot be verified from this frontend repository.

## I. Endpoint documentation gaps and the reported 222 routes

Counts use the current copied Chapter 14 table and selected supporting chapters listed in the prompt. The handoff route table is the denominator. ‘Exact path’ below is a literal index path string occurrence; ‘method/path pair’ requires the method and that literal path to appear on one source line within 120 characters. This conservative source-text measure can miss prose that documents a route family without repeating a literal URL; it does not evaluate DTO completeness.

| Measure | Current count |
|---|---:|
| Chapter 14 table rows / unique method-path pairs | 595 / 595 |
| Chapter 14 prose registration count | 594 |
| Table classifications: first-batch frontend-facing | 333 |
| Table classifications: first-batch staff-facing | 79 |
| Table first-batch total | 412 |
| First-batch exact literal path mentioned elsewhere in selected handoff | 291/412 |
| First-batch exact literal method/path pair on one line elsewhere | 277/412 |
| First-batch with no exact method/path pair by this method | 135/412 |

| Chapter 15 literal route-path references | 11/595 (11/412 first-batch) |

The Pass 13 `222/411` result (189 exact-path mentions) is historical and does not reproduce against the current listed files using this method. The current table also has a different denominator and the current handoff has been revised. This does **not** mean the 135 first-batch route pairs lacking a literal same-line mention are all blocked; inspect their `Contract guide`. Likewise, the Chapter 15/16 path counts undercount family-level DTO/response contracts. The authoritative conclusion is: **endpoint method/path matching is complete for current frontend calls; route-specific DTO/security completeness varies and still requires Chapters 01–20 and backend acceptance.**

## M. Readiness for Pass 15

**Pass 14 verdict: PARTIAL.** All frontend-discovered HTTP route patterns matched the current Chapter 14 method/path inventory, and the two identified frontend-owned protected-document/title-step-up issues were fixed and tested. The 595-versus-594 table discrepancy, 333-versus-332 first-batch-facing discrepancy, route rows without direct UI calls, nonuniform DTO/response documentation, contract blockers, configuration/deployment dependencies and lack of live backend evidence prevent a COMPLETE verdict.

Pass 15 should focus on the actual schema definitions in Chapter 15, map each response wrapper to its DTO, nullable/optional fields and version tokens, and validate the selectively listed Chapter 16 response variations. It should not treat a route-path mention as proof that the DTO is complete. No live backend acceptance was performed in Pass 14.
